```text
88	We use hand-crafted statistical features rather than learned representations for three reasons: (1) they are directly interpretable, enabling degradation ordering analysis across model families; (2) they transfer across generators without retraining the feature extractor; and (3) they permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties, which is essential for understanding \emph{how} the depth signal degrades.
89	
90	\subsection{Ordinal Binary Decomposition Classifier}
91	\label{sec:classifier}
92	
93	Generational depth is inherently ordinal: depth~1 is closer to depth~0 than depth~3 is.
94	We exploit this structure via \emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), which decomposes a $(K{+}1)$-class ordinal classification problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers.
95	The $k$-th sub-classifier estimates:
96	\begin{equation}
97	  P(\text{depth} \leq k \mid \mathbf{x}), \quad k = 0, 1, \ldots, K{-}1,
98	\end{equation}
99	where $\mathbf{x} \in \mathbb{R}^{15}$ is the feature vector.
100	Each sub-classifier is an independent LightGBM model.
101	The predicted depth is the smallest $k$ for which the cumulative probability exceeds 0.5:
102	\begin{equation}
103	  \hat{d}(\mathbf{x}) = \min\{k : P(\text{depth} \leq k \mid \mathbf{x}) > 0.5\}.
104	\end{equation}
105	
106	We compared OBD-LightGBM against two alternatives: standard (unordered) multiclass LightGBM and ordinal logistic regression (LogisticAT).
107	OBD-LightGBM achieves lower mean absolute error (MAE) than multiclass LightGBM across all three model families (Qwen-rewrite: 0.984 vs.\ 1.016; Pythia: 1.107 vs.\ 1.140; OLMo: 1.147 vs.\ 1.181).
```

_source line 1878 · 2026-05-13T19:36:25.484Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
No OBD audit artifact found. The worker probably just checked the code and either found no issues or reported back to the Director without creating an artifact. But I've now independently verified the OBD implementation direction. Let me summarize what I found and post to Director.

The key findings:

1. **Direction**: Code uses `P(Y > k)` (lines 90, 92-93, 100), paper writes `P(depth ≤ k)` (method.tex line 97). These are complementary: `P(depth ≤ k) = 1 - P(Y > k)`. This is a **cosmetic** difference — either convention is fine as long as it's consistent.


2. **Prediction formula**: The code takes the argmax of class probabilities to get the mode, but the paper describes using the median—the smallest k where the cumulative probability exceeds 0.5. These aren't equivalent for non-unimodal distributions, so there's a real mismatch between what the paper claims and what the implementation actually does.

3. **Impact on reported metrics**: The reported MAE and accuracy numbers were generated using the code's mode-based approach, not the paper's median formula, making them internally consistent with the implementation but inconsistent with the paper's description. This is a reproducibility issue rather than a critical flaw—the core K* results rely on pairwise AUC scores that don't depend on the prediction method at all, and mode versus median predictions likely produce similar results in practice. Still, someone trying to reproduce from the paper alone would end up with different predictions than the actual code produced.
```

</details>

````json
{
  "text": "## OBD 方向审计 — 独立核实结果\n\nWorker 审计已完成，我独立读取了 `run_exp010_ordinal_comparison.py` 源码核实。发现两个不一致：\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **OBD 累积方向不一致：代码 P(Y>k) vs 论文 P(depth ≤ k)**\n  代码 `run_exp010_ordinal_comparison.py:90-93`:\n  ```\n  \"\"\"Ordinal Binary Decomposition: K-1 binary LGBMs for P(Y > k).\"\"\"\n  cum_probs[:, k] = P(Y > k)\n  y_bin = (y > k).astype(int)  # line 100\n  ```\n  论文 method.tex 第 97 行写 `P(depth ≤ k | x)`。两者是互补概率：P(depth ≤ k) = 1 - P(Y > k)。这是符号约定差异，数学上等价，但论文描述与代码不匹配影响可复现性。\n  **建议**：论文改为 P(depth > k) 与代码一致（这是 OBD 文献的主流约定），或代码改为 P(depth ≤ k)。\n\n- [severity: warning] **预测公式不一致：代码 argmax vs 论文 median**\n  代码 `run_exp010_ordinal_comparison.py:123`:\n  ```python\n  preds = class_probs.argmax(axis=1)  # 分布的 mode\n  ```\n  论文 method.tex 第 103 行:\n  ```\n  d̂(x) = min{k : P(depth ≤ k | x) > 0.5}  # 分布的 median\n  ```\n  Mode ≠ median（反例：P = [0.1, 0.4, 0.1, 0.4] → argmax=1, median=2）。纸面上描述了一种算法，实际运行了另一种。\n\n  **影响评估**：K* 核心结论不受影响（K* 基于 pairwise AUC，不涉及硬预测）。MAE/accuracy 是次要指标，受此影响但实际偏差可能很小（15D 特征+6 类场景下 mode 和 median 高度重合）。但这是可复现性问题——按论文复现会得到不同的 MAE/accuracy 值。\n  \n  **建议**：论文预测公式改为 argmax 以匹配代码，或两者都改为 median（ordinal 分类文献中 median 更标准）。\n\n### 未发现问题\n- FEAT_COLS 15 维与论文 Table 1 完全一致 ✓\n- GroupKFold(n_splits=5) 与论文描述一致 ✓\n- LightGBM 超参（n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, lr=0.05, num_leaves=31）在合理范围 ✓\n\n### 总结\n两个 WARNING 均为论文-代码一致性问题，不影响核心结论 K*=2，但需在提交前修正以避免审稿人质疑可复现性。",
````

```text
- 0v1: 0.9982
- 1v2: 0.7039
- 2v3: 0.5823
- 3v4: 0.5293
- 4v5: 0.5237

K* = 2 (1v2 = 0.7039 > 0.60, 2v3 = 0.5823 < 0.60)

This is interesting - T=1.2 gives the highest 1v2 AUC of all configs:


- Config 1 (greedy): 1v2 = 0.6893
- Config 2 (nuc p=0.9): 1v2 = 0.6866
- Config 3 (T=0.7): 1v2 = 0.6887
- Config 4 (T=1.2): 1v2 = 0.7039

The higher temperature is producing the strongest signal between generations - the added randomness seems to amplify how much quality degrades from one generation to the next. K* stays at 2 since the 2v3 comparison still falls below the threshold, but this temperature setting is clearly capturing the degradation pattern better than the other approaches.

This aligns with D016's claim that all configurations converge on K*=2. Now I want to look at the feature importance and ordinal results for the T=1.2 config to see what's driving these differences.
```

```text
Good. Config 3 full data: 0v1=0.9878, 1v2=0.6887, 2v3=0.5902, 3v4=0.5393, 4v5=0.5349. K*=2. The registry just has incomplete data for config 3.

Now let me compile the full summary for all 4 configs:

| Config | Decoding | 0v1 | 1v2 | 2v3 | K* |


|--------|----------|-----|-----|-----|-----|
| 1 greedy | greedy | 0.9797 | 0.6893 | 0.5613 | 2 |
| 2 nuc09 | p=0.9, T=1.0 | 0.9946 | 0.6866 | 0.5917 | 2 |
| 3 t07 | p=0.95, T=0.7 | 0.9878 | 0.6887 | 0.5902 | 2 |
| 4 t12 | p=0.95, T=1.2 | 0.9982 | 0.7039 | 0.5823 | 2 |

Looking at the results, config 4 with temperature 1.2 performs best on the first metric, while config 3 maintains the strongest consistency across the middle metrics. All configs converge on K*=2.

The 2v3 scores are clustering tightly around 0.59, with nuc09 and t07 both pushing close to 0.60—that's worth noting since they're the strongest performers on that metric. The 1v2 range is remarkably narrow across all configs, suggesting the signal separation is fairly stable regardless of the sampling strategy.

But here's the key point: K* determination relies on the confidence interval lower bound exceeding 0.60, not the point estimate itself, so even though some of these 2v3 values are hovering near the threshold, the actual K* assignments should hold up. And the T=1.2 config, which D016 flagged as a potential edge case that might push K* to 3, actually shows 2v3=0.5823—well below the cutoff. Now I need to verify whether all these configs are actually running in rewrite mode as the experiment description claims.
```
